### **Scientific Strategy Report: Viscous Burgers Equation Operator Mapping**

#### **1. Data Source**
- **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
- **Instruction:** The Planner must verify the keys in the `.mat` file (typically `u`, `x`, `t` or similar) and ensure the initial condition $u(x, t=0)$ is extracted as the Branch input ($F$) and the full trajectory $u(x, t)$ as the target ($S$).

#### **2. Problem Statement**
- **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers Equation: $\frac{\partial u}{\partial t} + u\frac{\partial u}{\partial x} = \nu \frac{\partial^2 u}{\partial x^2}$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
- **Operator Mapping:** This is a **Function-to-Trajectory** problem where $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$. The model maps the initial condition $u_0(x)$ to the full spatio-temporal solution $u(x, t)$.
- **Boundary Conditions:** Periodic in $x$.

#### **3. Input (F_space)**
- **Domain dimension (d):** 1D ($x \in [0, 1]$).
- **Value dimension (k):** Scalar ($u$).
- **Structure:** Grid-based discretization of the initial condition.

#### **4. Output (S_space)**
- **Domain dimension (d_y):** 2D ($x \in [0, 1], t \in [0, 1]$).
- **Value dimension (q):** Scalar ($u$).
- **Characteristics:** Periodic in space. Low viscosity ($\nu=0.001$) suggests the potential for steep gradients or shock-like features that require robust feature representation.

#### **5. Normalization (In-Graph)**
- **Trunk (t, x):** Normalize coordinates to **$[-1, 1]$** inside the model's `apply` function.
- **Branch (u0):** Normalize the input function to **$[-1, 1]$** inside the model's `apply` function.
- **Activation Alignment:** Since we are using `nn.tanh` for the baseline, the $[-1, 1]$ range is optimal.

#### **6. Architecture (Strategy 1: Baseline DeepONet)**
- **Methodology:** `Standard_DeepONet`
- **7a. Branch:**
    - **Type:** Standard MLP.
    - **Architecture:** `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]` (Sizing Rule 1).
    - **Activation:** `nn.tanh`.
- **7b. Trunk:**
    - **Type:** Standard MLP.
    - **Architecture:** `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]` (Matches Branch embedding).
    - **Activation:** `nn.tanh`.
    - **Enhancements:** Because the problem is periodic, the Planner **MUST** implement **Periodic Fourier Features** for the spatial component ($x$) of the Trunk. 
        - Parameters: `kmax=5`, `L=2.0` (corresponding to the normalized $[-1, 1]$ domain).

#### **7. Loss Function**
- **Lambda (vRBA):** Use a single learnable Lambda of shape $(N, P)$ to weight the points.
- **Error_fn:** Mean Squared Error (MSE).
- **Weights (W):** Scalar weight $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$ to scale the loss based on the target magnitude.
- **Formula:** `mean(W * (Lambda * (S_pred - S_true))**2)`

#### **8. Optimization & Hyperparameters**
- **Iterations:** Set `max_iterations = 100000`.
- **Learning Rate:** 
    - Branch Optimizer: Initial `1e-3`.
    - Trunk Optimizer: Initial `1e-3`.
- **Schedule:** `decay_rate = 0.99`, `decay_steps = 2500`.

- Standard DeepONet

**Enhancements:**
- Periodic Fourier Features (for Trunk spatial input)

**Explanation:**
The `Periodic_Fourier_Features` class is extracted to handle the periodic boundary conditions of the Viscous Burgers Equation. It maps the spatial input into a deterministic periodic feature space using sine and cosine functions based on the specified maximum wave number (`kmax`) and domain period (`Lx`). This class is self-contained and does not require any additional custom dependencies from the library.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

New Code (lines 3-17):
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper

class DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    kmax: int
    L: float
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    
    def setup(self):
        self.branch_net = MLP(features=self.branch_layers, 
                                activation=self.activation)
        self.trunk_net = MLP(features=self.trunk_layers, 
                               activation=self.activation)
                               
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        # 1. In-graph Normalization (Section 5)
        F_min, F_max = jnp.min(F), jnp.max(F)
        F_norm = 2.0 * (F - F_min) / (F_max - F_min + 1e-8) - 1.0
        
        y_norm = 2.0 * y - 1.0 # Maps [0, 1] to [-1, 1]
        
        # 2. Branch
        B = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        
        # 3. Trunk with Periodic Fourier Features (Section 7b)
        t = y_norm[:, 0:1]
        x = y_norm[:, 1:2]
        x_per = Periodic_Fourier_Features(kmax=self.kmax, Lx=self.L)(x)
        trunk_input = jnp.concatenate([t, x_per], axis=1)
        
        T = self.trunk_net(trunk_input).T
        
        # 4. Prediction
        U_pred = B @ T
        return U_pred
```

Cell 10: The vRBA-Weighted Loss Function
- Action: Update the `loss_all` function signature to include the scalar weight `W` as required by Section 7 of the [OVERALL_STRATEGY_REPORT].
- Action: Implement the weighted loss formula: `mean(W * (Lambda * (S_pred - S_true))**2)`.

New Code (lines 3-10):
```python
def loss_all(params, apply_fn, F, y, U, Lambda_batch, it, current_gamma, eta, 
             spatial_update_fn_baked, W):  
    # Get model prediction using the apply_fn 
    outputs = apply_fn(params, F, y)     
    R = outputs - U.T 
    # Call the baked-in function to update vRBA weights
    new_Lambda_batch = spatial_update_fn_baked(R, Lambda_batch, it, 
                                              current_gamma, eta) 
    # Compute the weighted loss using scalar weight W (Section 7)
    loss = jnp.mean(W * (new_Lambda_batch * R)**2) 
    return loss, new_Lambda_batch 
```

Cell 11: Gradient and Update Functions

New Code (lines 4-14):
```python
@partial(jit, static_argnums=(1, 2, 9)) # loss_fn, apply_fn, baked_fn are static
def compute_grads_and_loss(params, loss_fn, apply_fn, 
                           F, y, U, Lambda_batch, it, current_gamma, 
                           spatial_update_fn_baked, W): 
     
    def loss_wrapper(p):
        return loss_fn(p, apply_fn, F, y, U, Lambda_batch, it, 
                       current_gamma, eta, spatial_update_fn_baked, W)             
    (loss_value, new_Lambda_batch), g = jax.value_and_grad(loss_wrapper, has_aux=True)(params)
     
    return loss_value, new_Lambda_batch, g
```

--- Strategy Report Received ---
**Scientific Strategy Report: Solving Viscous Burgers Equation with KAN-DeepONet**

### 0. Triage & Strategy Selection
*   **User Request:** Solve viscous Burgers ($\nu=0.001$) with periodic BCs, mapping $u_0(x) \to u(t, x)$.
*   **Advisor Recommendation:** The previous MLP-based attempt (Strategy 1) failed with 25.6% error and instability. The Advisor recommends **Strategy 2: Standard DeepONet + KAN Trunk** using a **Fourier basis** to handle the periodicity and steep gradients.
*   **Selected Path:** Path 2 (Advisor Recommendation).

### 1. Data Source
*   **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
*   **Instruction:** The Planner must load the `.mat` file and extract the initial conditions as the Branch input ($F$) and the full space-time solution as the target ($S$).

### 2. Problem Statement
*   **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers: $\partial u/\partial t + u\partial u/\partial x = \nu\partial^2 u/\partial x^2$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
*   **Operator Mapping:** Mapping initial state $u(x, t=0)$ to the trajectory $u(x, t \in [0, 1])$.
*   **Type:** This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem where $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

### 3. Input ($F_{space}$)
*   **Domain dimension:** 1D ($x$).
*   **Value dimension:** Scalar ($u_0$).
*   **Structure:** Discretized grid of the initial condition.

### 4. Output ($S_{space}$)
*   **Domain dimension:** 2D ($t, x$).
*   **Value dimension:** Scalar ($u$).
*   **Characteristics:** Periodic in $x$, containing high-frequency features and steep gradients (shocks) due to the low viscosity ($\nu=1000^{-1}$).

### 5. Normalization (In-Graph)
*   **CRITICAL INSTRUCTION:** All normalization must occur inside the model's `__call__` method.
*   **Trunk (per-dimension):** Normalize $(t, x)$ coordinates to **$[-1, 1]$** (required for Fourier-basis KAN).
*   **Branch (global):** Normalize the input function $u_0$ to **$[-1, 1]$** (consistent with `nn.tanh` activation).
*   **Implementation:** The Planner must pass the min/max bounds of the training data to the model as static arguments to perform this normalization differentiably.

### 6. Architecture (Strategy 2)
*   **Methodology:** `Standard_DeepONet`
*   **6a. Branch:**
    *   **Type:** Standard MLP.
    *   **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 1: `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`.
    *   **Activation:** `nn.tanh`.
*   **6b. Trunk:**
    *   **Type:** KAN (Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
    *   **Basis:** `fourier` (as recommended by the Advisor for periodic steep gradients).
    *   **Architecture:** Following Sizing Rule 2. With a degree of 7 ($\sqrt{7+1} \approx 2.82$), the hidden layers should be $\approx 100 / 2.82 \approx 35$.
    *   **Instruction:** Use `layers=[35, 35, 35, 35, 120]` and `degree=7`.
*   **Iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=100000`.

### 7. Loss Function
*   **Lambda (vRBA):** Use a single `Lambda(N, P)` for variational Residual Based Attention.
*   **Error_fn:** `MSE`.
*   **Weights (W):** Scalar weight $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$ to normalize the loss scale.
*   **Formula:** `mean(W * (Lambda * (S_pred - S_true))**2)`

### 8. Optimization (Learning Rate)
*   **Branch Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `1e-3`.
*   **Trunk Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `3.33e-4` (Default for KAN Trunk).
*   **Schedule:** Use the default decay: `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500`.

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires a KAN architecture with a Fourier basis for the trunk network to handle periodic boundary conditions and steep gradients. The main `KAN` class is extracted from the library, along with its standard basis layer dependencies (`RBF_KAN_layer`, `Sin_KAN_layer`, `Polynomial_KAN_layer`, and `Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer`). Since a specific Fourier basis layer was not present in the library, a `Fourier_KAN_layer` was generated using `RBF_KAN_layer` as a template. This generated layer computes sine and cosine expansions of the input features to form the required periodic basis.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### RBF_KAN_layer
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### Sin_KAN_layer
```python
class Sin_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int = 5
    grid_min: float = -0.1
    grid_max: float = 0.1
    normalization: callable = jnp.sin ## This should not be changed!!! otherwise we need other normalization
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers=self.degree
        self.centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        X = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        p_i = self.centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)
        W_i = self.param(
            'W_i',
            nn.initializers.normal(1),
            (p_i.shape)
        )
        mu=jnp.exp(-(W_i**2)/2)*self.normalization_fn(p_i)
        sigma=jnp.sqrt(0.5-0.5*jnp.exp(-(2*W_i**2)*jnp.cos(2*p_i)-mu**2))+1e-12
        b_i=(self.normalization_fn(W_i*X+p_i)-mu)/sigma
        b_i = jnp.transpose(b_i, (0, 2, 1))
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', b_i, C_n)
        return output
```

### Polynomial_KAN_layer
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
```python
class Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    num_centers: int = 5
    grid_min: float = -0.5
    grid_max: float = 0.5
    polynomial_type: str='T'
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        X = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        b = self.centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)
        W_p = self.param(
            'W_p',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (b.shape)
        )
        X=self.normalization_fn(W_p*X+b)
        X=jnp.sum(X,axis=1)
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                         (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # In,Out,Degree+1
        T_n= jnp.stack([self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(self.degree + 1)],axis=1)
        C_n_Tn = jnp.einsum("bdi,iod->bo", T_n, C_n)
        return C_n_Tn
```

## GENERATED FUNCTION TREAT WITH CAUTION
### Fourier_KAN_layer
```python
class Fourier_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    
    def setup(self):
        self.num_frequencies = self.degree
        self.normalization_fn = self.normalization
        
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        
        # Frequencies k = 1, 2, ..., degree
        k = jnp.arange(1, self.num_frequencies + 1, dtype=jnp.float32)
        
        X_expanded = X[:, :, None]             # Shape: (batch_size, in_dim, 1)
        k_expanded = k[None, None, :]          # Shape: (1, 1, num_frequencies)
        
        # Compute theta = pi * k * x (assuming normalized inputs in [-1, 1])
        theta = jnp.pi * X_expanded * k_expanded # Shape: (batch_size, in_dim, num_frequencies)
        
        sin_basis = jnp.sin(theta)
        cos_basis = jnp.cos(theta)
        
        # Concatenate sin and cos basis functions
        basis = jnp.concatenate([sin_basis, cos_basis], axis=-1) # Shape: (batch_size, in_dim, 2 * num_frequencies)
        
        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_frequencies * 2)),
            (in_dim, self.num_frequencies * 2, self.out_dim)
        )
        
        # Einsum to compute linear combination of basis functions
        output = jnp.einsum('bif,ifo->bo', basis, C_n)
        
        return output
```

### KAN
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

```python
class Fourier_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    
    def setup(self):
        self.num_frequencies = self.degree
        self.normalization_fn = self.normalization
        
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        
        # Frequencies k = 1, 2, ..., degree
        k = jnp.arange(1, self.num_frequencies + 1, dtype=jnp.float32)
        
        X_expanded = X[:, :, None]             # Shape: (batch_size, in_dim, 1)
        k_expanded = k[None, None, :]          # Shape: (1, 1, num_frequencies)
        
        # Compute theta = pi * k * x (assuming normalized inputs in [-1, 1])
        theta = jnp.pi * X_expanded * k_expanded # Shape: (batch_size, in_dim, num_frequencies)
        
        sin_basis = jnp.sin(theta)
        cos_basis = jnp.cos(theta)
        
        # Concatenate sin and cos basis functions
        basis = jnp.concatenate([sin_basis, cos_basis], axis=-1) # Shape: (batch_size, in_dim, 2 * num_frequencies)
        
        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_frequencies * 2)),
            (in_dim, self.num_frequencies * 2, self.out_dim)
        )
        
        # Einsum to compute linear combination of basis functions
        output = jnp.einsum('bif,ifo->bo', basis, C_n)
        
        return output

class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf':
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'fourier':
            self.basis_layer_class = Fourier_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x

class DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    kan_degree: int
    f_min: float
    f_max: float
    y_min: jnp.ndarray
    y_max: jnp.ndarray
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh

    def setup(self):
        self.branch_net = MLP(features=self.branch_layers, 
                              activation=self.activation)
        self.trunk_net = KAN(layers=self.trunk_layers, 
                             degree=self.kan_degree, 
                             basis_type='fourier')

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        # 1. In-graph Normalization (Section 5)
        F_norm = 2.0 * (F - self.f_min) / (self.f_max - self.f_min + 1e-8) - 1.0
        y_norm = 2.0 * (y - self.y_min) / (self.y_max - self.y_min + 1e-8) - 1.0
        
        # 2. Branch
        B = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        
        # 3. Trunk (KAN)
        T = self.trunk_net(y_norm).T
        
        # 4. Prediction
        U_pred = B @ T
        return U_pred
```

- New Code (lines 1-11):
```python
class MLP(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.features[:-1]:
            x = nn.Dense(feat)(x)
            x = self.activation(x)
        x = nn.Dense(self.features[-1])
        return x

class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output

class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

# Scientific Strategy Report: Viscous Burgers Equation (RBF-KAN Refinement)

## 0. Triage & Strategy Selection
- **Problem Type:** Viscous Burgers Equation ($\nu=0.001$) with periodic boundary conditions.
- **Current Status:** Scientific failure in Trial 2 (13.86% error) using a Fourier-basis KAN.
- **Advisor Recommendation:** The Advisor noted scattered "hotspots" of error and training instabilities. The recommendation is to **escalate Strategy 2 (Standard DeepONet + KAN Trunk)** by switching the basis function from `fourier` to **`RBF`** (Radial Basis Functions) to better handle localized error patches.
- **Strategy:** **Strategy 2: Standard DeepONet + KAN Trunk (RBF Basis)**.

## 1. Data Source
- **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
- **Instruction:** The Planner must load the initial conditions as the Branch input ($F$) and the full space-time solution as the target ($S$).

## 2. Problem Statement
- **Governing Equation:** Burgers Equation: $\partial u/\partial t + u\partial u/\partial x = \nu\partial^2 u/\partial x^2$ where $\nu = 0.001$.
- **Operator Mapping:** Mapping the initial state $u(x, t=0)$ to the trajectory $u(x, t)$ for $t \in [0, 1]$.
- **Type:** This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem where $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

## 3. Input (F_space)
- **Domain:** 1D spatial domain $x \in [0, 1]$.
- **Value Dimension:** Scalar ($k=1$).
- **Structure:** Discretized initial condition $u(x, 0)$.

## 4. Output (S_space)
- **Domain:** 2D space-time domain $(x, t) \in [0, 1] \times [0, 1]$.
- **Value Dimension:** Scalar ($q=1$).
- **Characteristics:** Periodic boundary conditions. Low viscosity ($\nu=0.001$) leads to the formation of sharp gradients/shocks. The Advisor identified scattered error hotspots, suggesting the need for local basis functions.

## 5. Normalization (In-Graph)
- **Trunk (t, x):** 
    - **Instruction:** Normalize Trunk inputs $(t, x)$ to **$[0, 1]$** inside the model's `apply` function. This is required for the **RBF basis**.
- **Branch (u(x, 0)):**
    - **Instruction:** Normalize the Branch input function $F$ to **$[-1, 1]$** inside the model's `apply` function (consistent with `nn.tanh`).
- **Implementation:** The Planner must ensure `t_min, t_max, x_min, x_max` and `u_min, u_max` are computed from the training data and passed as static arguments to the model.

## 6. Architecture

### 6a. Branch Network
- **Type:** Standard MLP.
- **Architecture:** Following **Sizing Rule 1**, use 6 layers: `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`.
- **Activation:** `nn.tanh`.

### 6b. Trunk Network
- **Type:** KAN (Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
- **Basis:** **RBF** (Radial Basis Function).
- **Architecture:** Following **Sizing Rule 2** (with `degree=5` for RBF, $\sqrt{6} \approx 2.45$):
    - MLP Baseline: `[100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`
    - KAN Width: $120 / 2.45 \approx 49$.
    - **Instruction:** Use a KAN with layers `[50, 50, 50, 50, 120]`.
- **Output Dim:** 120 (must match Branch).

## 7. Loss Function
- **Methodology:** Standard DeepONet.
- **Lambda (vRBA):** Single Lambda $(N, P)$ to weight points.
- **Error Function:** MSE.
- **Weights (W):** $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$ to normalize the scale of the solution.
- **Iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations = 100000`.

## 8. Optimization (Learning Rate)
- **Branch Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `1e-3`.
- **Trunk Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `3.33e-4` (scaled for KAN).
- **Schedule:** Use `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500`.

Standard DeepONet

Enhancements:
KAN with RBF basis

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires a Kolmogorov-Arnold Network (KAN) utilizing a Radial Basis Function (RBF) basis to handle localized error hotspots in the Burgers equation. The `KAN` class acts as the main trunk architecture, which dynamically selects its basis layer. To support this, the `RBF_KAN_layer` dependency is extracted, which implements the trainable Gaussian radial basis functions for the univariate activations.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### RBF_KAN_layer
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### KAN
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

```python
class DeepONetModel(nn.Module): # LINE_73
    branch_layers: Sequence[int] # LINE_74
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int] # LINE_75
    kan_degree: int # LINE_76
    f_min: float # LINE_77
    f_max: float # LINE_78
    y_min: jnp.ndarray # LINE_79
    y_max: jnp.ndarray # LINE_80
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh # LINE_81
    def setup(self): # LINE_82
        self.branch_net = MLP(features=self.branch_layers, # LINE_83
                              activation=self.activation) # LINE_84
        self.trunk_net = KAN(layers=self.trunk_layers, # LINE_85
                             degree=self.kan_degree, # LINE_86
                             basis_type='rbf') # LINE_87
    @nn.compact # LINE_88
    def __call__(self, F, y): # LINE_89
        F_norm = 2.0 * (F - self.f_min) / (self.f_max - self.f_min + 1e-8) - 1.0 # LINE_90
        y_norm = (y - self.y_min) / (self.y_max - self.y_min + 1e-8) # LINE_91
        B = self.branch_net(F_norm) # LINE_92
        T = self.trunk_net(y_norm).T # LINE_93
        U_pred = B @ T # LINE_94
        return U_pred # LINE_95
```

--- Strategy Report Received ---
### **Scientific Strategy Report: Viscous Burgers Equation (Operator Mapping)**

#### **0. Triage & Strategy Selection**
*   **Problem:** Viscous Burgers Equation ($\nu = 0.001$) with periodic boundary conditions.
*   **Goal:** Map initial condition $u(x, 0)$ to the full trajectory $u(x, t)$.
*   **Advisor Recommendation:** The previous attempt using a KAN Trunk (RBF basis) failed to reach the 5% error threshold (11.56% achieved). The Advisor recommends escalating to **Strategy 3: Standard DeepONet with KKAN Trunk and eMLP Branch**, using an **RBF basis** for the outer KKAN layer.

#### **1. Data Source**
*   **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
*   **Instruction:** The Planner must verify the keys (likely `u`, `x`, `t`) and ensure the initial condition is extracted as the input function $F$ and the full spatio-temporal solution as the target $S$.

#### **2. Problem Statement**
*   **Governing Equation:** Burgers Equation: $\frac{\partial u}{\partial t} + u\frac{\partial u}{\partial x} = \nu\frac{\partial^2 u}{\partial x^2}$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
*   **Operator Mapping:** Mapping $u_0(x) \to u(t, x)$.
*   **Type:** This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem where $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

#### **3. Input ($F_{space}$)**
*   **Domain Dimension ($d$):** 1D ($x$).
*   **Value Dimension ($k$):** Scalar ($u$).
*   **Structure:** Grid-based initial conditions.

#### **4. Output ($S_{space}$)**
*   **Domain Dimension ($d_y$):** 2D ($t, x$).
*   **Value Dimension ($q$):** Scalar ($u$).
*   **Characteristics:** Periodic boundary conditions. At $\nu=0.001$, the solution develops steep gradients (shocks) that require high-expressivity features to resolve.

#### **5. Normalization (In-Graph)**
*   **CRITICAL:** All normalization must be implemented inside the model's `apply` function.
*   **Trunk ($t, x$):** Normalize coordinates to **$[0, 1]$** (required for RBF-based KKAN).
*   **Branch ($u_0$):** Normalize input function values to **$[0, 1]$** (consistent with eMLP/ReLU-like scaling).
*   **Instruction for Planner:** Pass the training data min/max values as static arguments to the model to perform `(x - min) / (max - min)` inside the `__call__` method.

#### **6. Architecture (Strategy 3: KKAN + eMLP)**
*   **Methodology:** `Standard_DeepONet`
*   **6a. Branch:**
    *   **Type:** `eMLP` (Enhanced MLP with internal polynomial expansions).
    *   **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 3**. Set `layers = [32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree = 7`.
*   **6b. Trunk:**
    *   **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
    *   **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 3**.
        *   **Outer KAN:** `output_dim = 101`, `degree = 5`, `basis = "RBF"` (as per Advisor).
        *   **Inner eMLPs:** `features = [32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]`, `degree_T = 5`.
*   **Enhancements:** None. The KKAN and eMLP architectures serve as the feature generators. Do not add Periodic Fourier Features unless explicitly requested.

#### **7. Loss Function**
*   **Type:** MSE.
*   **Lambda (vRBA):** Single `Lambda` of shape $(N, P)$ to weight points.
*   **Weights ($W$):** $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$ to normalize the scale of the solution.
*   **Formula:** `mean(W * (Lambda * (S_pred - S_true))**2)`

#### **8. Optimization & Hyperparameters**
*   **Iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations = 100000`.
*   **Learning Rate:**
    *   **Branch Optimizer:** Initial `learning_rate = 1e-3`.
    *   **Trunk Optimizer:** Initial `learning_rate = 3.33e-4` (scaled for KKAN).
*   **Schedule:** `decay_rate = 0.99`, `decay_steps = 2500`.

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires an `eMLP` for the Branch network and a `KKAN` for the Trunk network, with the outer layer of the KKAN utilizing an `RBF_KAN_layer`. 
- The `eMLP` relies on `Polynomial_Embedding_Layer` (renamed from `Polynomial_Embedding` to match the internal calls) for feature expansion, and uses `WN_layer` and `AdaptiveResNet` for its core residual blocks.
- The `KKAN` architecture uses `get_Psi` to process inputs through inner `eMLP`-style blocks, and then applies the `RBF_KAN_layer` to compute the final outer function. 
All necessary dependencies have been extracted and ordered sequentially so that foundational layers are defined before the complex architectures that call them.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Polynomial_Embedding_Layer]
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### [WN_layer]
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### [AdaptiveResNet]
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### [RBF_KAN_layer]
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### [get_Psi]
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

### [eMLP]
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### [KKAN]
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

```python
class DeepONetModel(nn.Module): # LINE_73
    branch_layers: Sequence[int] # LINE_74
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int] # LINE_75
    branch_degree: int # LINE_76
    trunk_degree: int # LINE_77

                              degree=self.branch_degree) # LINE_86
        self.trunk_net = KKAN(features=self.trunk_layers, # LINE_87
                             degree=self.trunk_degree, # LINE_88
                             degree_T=self.trunk_degree_T, # LINE_89
                             output_dim=self.trunk_layers[-1]) # LINE_90
    @nn.compact # LINE_91
    def __call__(self, F, y): # LINE_92
        # In-graph normalization to [0, 1] as per Strategy Section 5
        F_norm = (F - self.f_min) / (self.f_max - self.f_min + 1e-8) # LINE_93
        y_norm = (y - self.y_min) / (self.y_max - self.y_min + 1e-8) # LINE_94

        U_pred = B @ T # LINE_97
```

Cell 6: Function Approximators (The "Swappable" Modules)

- New Code (lines 1-60):
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n

class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b

class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H

class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )
        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)
        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)
        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))
        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))
        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)
        return output

class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(X)
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi

class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(x)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H

class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

--- Strategy Report Received ---
**Scientific Strategy Report: Solving Viscous Burgers Equation via KKAN-DeepONet**

### 0. Triage - Strategy Determination
*   **User Request:** Solve viscous Burgers ($\nu=0.001$) with periodic BCs, mapping $u(x, 0) \to u(x, t)$.
*   **Advisor Recommendation:** The previous run (Strategy 3: KKAN with RBF basis) reached 7.24% error. The Advisor recommends escalating within **Strategy 3** by switching the **KKAN basis to Fourier** to better capture the periodic nature of the problem.
*   **Path:** Path 2 (Advisor Recommendation).

### 1. Data Source
*   **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
*   **Instruction:** The Planner must extract the initial conditions as the Branch input ($F$) and the full space-time grid as the Trunk input ($y$).

### 2. Problem Statement
*   **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers Equation: $\partial u/\partial t + u\partial u/\partial x = \nu\partial^2 u/\partial x^2$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
*   **Operator Mapping:** Mapping the initial state function $u(x, t=0)$ to the trajectory $u(x, t)$ for $t \in [0, 1]$.
*   **Type:** This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem where $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

### 3. Input ($F_{space}$)
*   **Domain Dimension ($d$):** 1D ($x \in [0, 1]$).
*   **Value Dimension ($k$):** 1 (Scalar $u_0$).
*   **Structure:** Discretized grid of initial conditions.

### 4. Output ($S_{space}$)
*   **Domain Dimension ($d_y$):** 2D ($t, x$).
*   **Value Dimension ($q$):** 1 (Scalar $u$).
*   **Characteristics:** Periodic boundary conditions, high-frequency features/steep gradients due to low viscosity ($\nu=10^{-3}$).

### 5. Normalization (In-Graph)
*   **CRITICAL:** All normalization must be implemented *inside* the model's `__call__` method.
*   **Trunk ($t, x$):** Normalize to **$[-1, 1]$** (Required for Fourier basis).
*   **Branch ($u_0$):** Normalize to **$[0, 1]$** (Required for eMLP).
*   **Instruction for Planner:** Pass the min/max values of the training data as static attributes to the model to perform `2.0 * (x - x_min) / (x_max - x_min) - 1.0` for the Trunk and `(F - F_min) / (F_max - F_min)` for the Branch.

### 6. Architecture (Strategy 3: Refined)
*   **Methodology:** `Standard_DeepONet`
*   **7a. Branch:**
    *   **Type:** `eMLP`
    *   **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 3**.
    *   **Instruction:** Use `eMLP` with `layers=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree=7`.
*   **7b. Trunk:**
    *   **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
    *   **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 3**.
    *   **Instruction:** Use `KKAN` for the Trunk. Set `output_dim=101` and `degree=5` (for the outer KAN). For the inner `eMLP` features, use `features=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree_T=5`.
    *   **Basis:** **Fourier** (as recommended by the Advisor).
*   **Enhancements:** None. The KKAN and eMLP act as the feature generators.

### 7. Loss Function
*   **Type:** MSE.
*   **Lambda (vRBA):** Single Lambda (N, P) to weight points.
*   **Weights ($W$):** $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$.
*   **Instruction:** `Loss = mean(W * (Lambda * (S_pred - S_true))**2)`.

### 8. Optimization & Training
*   **Iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=100000`.
*   **Learning Rate:**
    *   Branch Optimizer: `1e-3`.
    *   Trunk Optimizer: `3.33e-4` (approx `1e-3 / 3`).
*   **Decay:** `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500`.

IMPLEMENTATION CODE REQUIRED:

Main Model:
Standard DeepONet

Enhancements:
eMLP (for Branch)
KKAN (for Trunk) with Fourier basis support

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires an `eMLP` for the Branch and a `KKAN` for the Trunk, utilizing a Fourier basis to capture the periodic boundary conditions of the viscous Burgers equation. 

Both `eMLP` and `KKAN` (via its inner `get_Psi` block) rely on `Polynomial_Embedding`, `WN_layer`, and `AdaptiveResNet` for their feature generation. The standard `KKAN` implementation in the library hardcodes an `RBF_KAN_layer` for its outer block, so it has been extracted as a dependency to ensure the code runs. 

However, to fulfill the "Fourier basis support" requirement, a custom `Fourier_KAN_layer` has been generated. The Planner will need to modify the `KKAN` class to use this new `Fourier_KAN_layer` instead of the default RBF layer. *(Note: The Planner will also need to ensure `Polynomial_Embedding` is correctly referenced as `Polynomial_Embedding_Layer` inside `eMLP` and `get_Psi` as per the original code's naming mismatch).*

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Polynomial_Embedding]
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### [WN_layer]
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### [AdaptiveResNet]
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### [RBF_KAN_layer]
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### [get_Psi]
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

## GENERATED FUNCTION TREAT WITH CAUTION
### [Fourier_KAN_layer]
```python
class Fourier_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    
    def setup(self):
        self.normalization_fn = self.normalization
        
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        
        # Frequencies: 1 to degree
        k = jnp.arange(1, self.degree + 1)
        
        # Expand X for broadcasting: (batch, in_dim, 1)
        X_expanded = X[:, :, None]
        
        # Compute sine and cosine features: (batch, in_dim, degree)
        sin_features = jnp.sin(jnp.pi * X_expanded * k)
        cos_features = jnp.cos(jnp.pi * X_expanded * k)
        
        # Concatenate features along the degree axis: (batch, in_dim, 2 * degree)
        features = jnp.concatenate([sin_features, cos_features], axis=-1)
        
        # Trainable coefficients
        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * 2 * self.degree)),
            (in_dim, 2 * self.degree, self.out_dim)
        )
        
        # Linear combination of features
        output = jnp.einsum('bid,ido->bo', features, C_n)
        return output
```

### [eMLP]
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### [KKAN]
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

New Code (lines 29-54):
```python
class DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int]
    branch_degree: int
    trunk_degree: int
    trunk_degree_T: int
    f_min: float
    f_max: float
    y_min: jnp.ndarray
    y_max: jnp.ndarray
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh

    def setup(self):
        self.branch_net = eMLP(layers=self.branch_layers,
                              degree=self.branch_degree)
        self.trunk_net = KKAN(features=self.trunk_layers,
                             degree=self.trunk_degree,
                             degree_T=self.trunk_degree_T,
                             output_dim=self.trunk_layers[-1])

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        # Normalization: Branch to [0, 1], Trunk to [-1, 1]
        F_norm = (F - self.f_min) / (self.f_max - self.f_min + 1e-8)
        y_norm = 2.0 * (y - self.y_min) / (self.y_max - self.y_min + 1e-8) - 1.0
        
        B = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        T = self.trunk_net(y_norm).T
        U_pred = B @ T
        return U_pred
```

New Code (lines 174-188):
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    degree_T: int = 5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int = 1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh

    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function (t, x)
        inputs = [X[:, 0:1], X[:, 1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        # Use Fourier basis for periodic Burgers equation
        sum_Phi = Fourier_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

--- Strategy Report Received ---
**Scientific Strategy Report: Viscous Burgers Equation Operator Learning**

### 0. Triage & Strategy Selection
- **User Request:** Solve the viscous Burgers equation ($\nu=1/1000$) mapping $u(x, 0) \to u(x, t)$ with periodic boundary conditions.
- **Advisor Recommendation:** The Advisor noted that Run 4 (Strategy 3: eMLP/KKAN with RBF basis) was the most successful attempt (7.24% error) but failed to meet the 5% threshold. The recommendation is to **re-run Strategy 3 (eMLP/KKAN with RBF basis) but switch the loss function from MSE to Relative L2 (R-L2)** to better capture the solution's scale and reduce high-frequency artifacts.
- **Selected Strategy:** **Strategy 3: Standard DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch (Refined with R-L2 Loss)**.

### 1. Data Source
- **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
- **Format:** `.mat` file. The Planner must extract the initial conditions $u_0(x)$ as the input function and the full trajectory $u(x, t)$ as the target.

### 2. Problem Statement
- **Governing Equation:** Burgers Equation: $\frac{\partial u}{\partial t} + u\frac{\partial u}{\partial x} = \nu\frac{\partial^2 u}{\partial x^2}$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
- **Operator Mapping:** This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem mapping $u(x, t=0) \to u(x, t)$.
- **Type:** $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

### 3. Input ($F_{space}$)
- **Domain Dimension ($d$):** 1D ($x$).
- **Value Dimension ($k$):** Scalar ($u$).
- **Structure:** Discrete grid of initial condition values.

### 4. Output ($S_{space}$)
- **Domain Dimension ($d_y$):** 2D ($x, t$).
- **Value Dimension ($q$):** Scalar ($u$).
- **Characteristics:** Periodic boundary conditions. The solution develops steep gradients (shocks) due to the low viscosity ($\nu=1/1000$), requiring high-capacity feature representation.

### 5. Normalization (In-Graph)
- **CRITICAL:** All normalization must be implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function.
- **Trunk (per-dimension):** Normalize coordinates $(t, x)$ to **$[0, 1]$** (required for RBF-based KKAN).
- **Branch (global):** Normalize the input function $F$ to **$[0, 1]$** (required for eMLP).
- **Instruction for Planner:** The model must compute the min/max from training data buffers and perform the transformation `(x - min) / (max - min)` inside the `__call__` method.

### 6. Architecture

**6a. Branch Network**
- **Type:** `eMLP` (Enhanced MLP with polynomial expansions).
- **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 3**.
- **Configuration:** `layers=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]`, `degree=7`.
- **Activation:** `nn.relu` (consistent with $[0, 1]$ normalization).

**6b. Trunk Network**
- **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
- **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 3**.
- **Configuration:** 
    - **Outer KAN:** `output_dim=101`, `degree=5`, `basis='rbf'`.
    - **Inner eMLP Features:** `features=[32, 32, 32, 32, 32, 101]`, `degree_T=5`.
- **Activation:** `nn.relu`.

### 7. Loss Function
- **Methodology:** Standard DeepONet.
- **Lambda (vRBA):** Single Lambda $(N, P)$ to weight the residual-based attention.
- **Error Function:** **Relative L2 (R-L2)**.
    - **Formula:** `mean((W * (Lambda * (S_pred - S_true))**2) / mean((W * S_true)**2))`
- **Weights (W):** $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$ to normalize the scale of the solution.

### 8. Optimization & Hyperparameters
- **Iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=100000`.
- **Learning Rate:**
    - **Branch Optimizer:** Initial `learning_rate = 1e-3`.
    - **Trunk Optimizer:** Initial `learning_rate = 3.33e-4` (approx $1e-3 / 3$).
- **Decay Schedule:** `decay_rate = 0.99`, `decay_steps = 2500`.

**Main Model:**
- `Standard DeepONet`

**Enhancements:**
- `eMLP` (for Branch)
- `KKAN` (for Trunk)
- `RBF basis` (for KKAN)
- `Relative L2 Loss` (R-L2)
- `vRBA` (Variational Residual Based Attention)

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `eMLP` architecture for the branch network and the `KKAN` architecture for the trunk network, utilizing an `RBF_KAN_layer` as its basis. Both `eMLP` and the inner block of `KKAN` (`get_Psi`) depend on `Polynomial_Embedding` for feature expansion, as well as `WN_layer` (Weight Normalization) and `AdaptiveResNet` (Adaptive Residual Connections) for their core multi-layer perceptron bodies. The `Relative L2 Loss` and `vRBA` enhancements are algorithmic components that do not have standalone classes in the library and will be implemented directly within the training loop by the Planner.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### Polynomial_Embedding
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### WN_layer
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### AdaptiveResNet
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### get_Psi
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

### RBF_KAN_layer
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### eMLP
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### KKAN
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

### 1. Triage - Strategy Determination
- **Specific Requests:** None found in `[USER_QUESTION]`.
- **Advisor Recommendation:** The `[ADVISOR_REPORT]` explicitly recommends escalating to **Strategy 4: QR-DeepONet with a baseline MLP Trunk/Branch**.
- **Path:** Path 2 (Advisor Recommendation).

### 2. Data Source
- **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
- **Instruction:** The Planner must verify the keys in the `.mat` file (typically `u`, `x`, `t`, or `usol`).

### 3. Problem Statement
- **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers Equation: $\partial u/\partial t + u\partial u/\partial x = \nu\partial^2 u/\partial x^2$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
- **Operator Mapping:** Mapping the initial condition $u(x, t=0)$ to the full spatio-temporal solution $u(x, t)$.
- **Type:** This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem where $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

### 4. Input (F_space)
- **Domain Dimension (d):** 1D ($x$).
- **Value Dimension (k):** Scalar ($u_0$).
- **Structure:** Grid-based initial conditions.

### 5. Output (S_space)
- **Domain Dimension (d_y):** 2D ($t, x$).
- **Value Dimension (q):** Scalar ($u$).
- **Characteristics:** Periodic boundary conditions. The solution exhibits steep gradients (shocks) due to the low viscosity ($\nu=1/1000$).

### 6. Normalization (In-Graph)
- **CRITICAL INSTRUCTION:** All normalization must be implemented *inside* the model's `__call__` method.
- **Trunk:** Normalize input coordinates $(t, x)$ to **$[-1, 1]$**.
- **Branch:** Normalize input function $u_0(x)$ to **$[-1, 1]$**.
- **Activations:** Use `nn.tanh` for both networks to remain consistent with the $[-1, 1]$ normalization range.

### 7. Architecture

**Strategy 4: QR-DeepONet (MLP Baseline)**
- **Methodology:** `QR_DeepONet`
- **7a. Branch:**
    - **Type:** Standard MLP.
    - **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 1**: `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`.
    - **Activation:** `nn.tanh`.
- **7b. Trunk:**
    - **Type:** Standard MLP.
    - **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 1**: `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`.
    - **Activation:** `nn.tanh`.
- **Enhancements:** None. As per Strategy 4, we are using a baseline MLP for the QR-decomposition approach.
- **Iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations = 200000`.

### 8. Loss Function
- **Type:** MSE (Standard for QR-DeepONet).
- **Lambda (vRBA):** Use QR-specific dual attention: $\Lambda_{spatial}$ (shape $N, P$) and $\Lambda_{latent}$ (shape $N, \text{embed\_dim}$).
- **Weights (W):** $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$ to scale the loss based on the magnitude of the solution.

### 9. Optimization (Learning Rate)
- **Initial Learning Rate:**
    - **Branch Optimizer:** `2e-3`.
    - **Trunk Optimizer:** `2e-3`.
- **Decay Schedule:** Use `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500`.

Cell 5: . Architecture QR-DeepONet
- Action: Implement in-graph normalization for both Branch and Trunk inputs in `apply_stage1`, `apply_stage2`, and `__call__` as per Section 6 of the report.
- Action: Ensure coordinates $(t, x)$ are mapped from $[0, 1]$ to $[-1, 1]$ and the input function $F$ is scaled to $[-1, 1]$.
- New Code (lines 21-41):
```python
    def apply_stage1(self, y):
        # Normalize coordinates (t, x) from [0, 1] to [-1, 1]
        y_norm = 2.0 * y - 1.0
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_norm)
        return Phi, self.A

    def apply_stage2(self, F):
        # Normalize input function to [-1, 1]
        f_min = jnp.min(F)
        f_max = jnp.max(F)
        F_norm = 2.0 * (F - f_min) / (f_max - f_min + 1e-8) - 1.0
        C = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        return C

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, F, y):
        """
        Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE.
        This uses the QR-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Q.T
        """
        # Normalize coordinates (t, x) from [0, 1] to [-1, 1]
        y_norm = 2.0 * y - 1.0
        # Normalize input function to [-1, 1]
        f_min = jnp.min(F)
        f_max = jnp.max(F)
        F_norm = 2.0 * (F - f_min) / (f_max - f_min + 1e-8) - 1.0
        
        C = self.branch_net(F_norm)
        Phi = self.trunk_net(y_norm)
         
        # Use JAX's QR decomposition
        Q, _ = jnp.linalg.qr(Phi, mode='reduced')
        U_pred = C @ Q.T
         
        return U_pred
```

Cell 6: Function Approximators (The "Swappable" Modules)
- Action: Change default activation function from `nn.relu` to `nn.tanh` as per Section 6 and 7 of the strategy report.
- New Code (lines 3-13):
```python
class MLP(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh

    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.features[:-1]:
            x = nn.Dense(feat)(x)
            x = self.activation(x)
        x = nn.Dense(self.features[-1])(x)
        return x
```

Cell 9: QR-DeepONet Loss Functions
