- Action: Modify function signatures to accept `W` and apply it to the weighted residual calculation.
- New Code (lines 3-30):
```python
def loss_stage1(params, apply_stage1_fn, y, U_all_T, Lambda_spatial_all,  
                it, current_gamma, eta, weight_update_fn_baked, W):
    """
    Computes the vRBA-weighted loss for Stage 1 (Trunk + A) using the FULL dataset.
    """
    Phi, A = apply_stage1_fn(params, y) # Shape: (N_points, embed_dim), (embed_dim, N_samples)
    Pred = Phi @ A # Shape: (N_points, N_samples)
    R1 = Pred - U_all_T     
    R1_T = R1.T # Shape: (N_samples, N_points)
    new_Lambda_spatial = weight_update_fn_baked(R1_T, Lambda_spatial_all, it, 
                                                current_gamma, eta)
     
    # Apply global scaling weight W and vRBA weights
    loss = jnp.mean((W * new_Lambda_spatial * R1_T)**2)
     
    return loss, new_Lambda_spatial

def loss_stage2(params, apply_stage2_fn, F_all, Target_all, Lambda_latent_all, 
                it, current_gamma, eta, weight_update_fn_baked, W):
    """
    Computes the vRBA-weighted loss for Stage 2 (Branch) using the FULL dataset.
    """
    C_all = apply_stage2_fn(params, F_all)
    R2 = C_all - Target_all
    new_Lambda_latent = weight_update_fn_baked(R2, Lambda_latent_all, it, 
                                               current_gamma, eta)
     
    # Apply global scaling weight W and vRBA weights
    loss = jnp.mean((W * new_Lambda_latent * R2)**2)     
    return loss, new_Lambda_latent
```

```python
@partial(jit, static_argnums=(1, 2, 8)) # loss_fn, apply_fn, baked_fn are static
def compute_grads_stage1(params, loss_fn, apply_fn, 
                         y, U_all_T, Lambda_spatial_all, 
                         it, current_gamma, 
                         weight_update_fn_baked, W):
     
    # This wrapper passes the arguments needed by loss_stage1
    def loss_wrapper(p):
        return loss_fn(p, apply_fn, y, U_all_T, Lambda_spatial_all, it, 
                       current_gamma, eta, weight_update_fn_baked, W)
     
    (loss_value, new_Lambda_spatial), g = jax.value_and_grad(loss_wrapper, has_aux=True)(params)
     
    return loss_value, new_Lambda_spatial, g

@partial(jit, static_argnums=(1, 2, 8)) # loss_fn, apply_fn, baked_fn are static
def compute_grads_stage2(params, loss_fn, apply_fn, 
                         F_all, Target_all, Lambda_latent_all, 
                         it, current_gamma, 
                         weight_update_fn_baked, W):
     
    def loss_wrapper(p):
        return loss_fn(p, apply_fn, F_all, Target_all, Lambda_latent_all, it, 
                       current_gamma, eta, weight_update_fn_baked, W)
     
    (loss_value, new_Lambda_latent), g = jax.value_and_grad(loss_wrapper, has_aux=True)(params)
     
    return loss_value, new_Lambda_latent, g
```

--- Strategy Report Received ---
### **Scientific Strategy Report: QR-DeepONet with Periodic Fourier Features for Viscous Burgers Equation**

#### **0. Triage & Strategy Selection**
- **User Request:** Solve Viscous Burgers ($\nu=0.001$) with periodic BCs.
- **Advisor Recommendation:** The previous run (Run 10) using a baseline QR-DeepONet (MLP) failed significantly (37.66% error). The Advisor recommends escalating **Strategy 4 (QR-DeepONet)** by adding **Periodic Fourier Features** to the spatial input of the trunk to enforce the periodic boundary conditions.
- **Path:** Path 2 (Advisor Recommendation).

#### **1. Data Source**
- **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
- **Instruction:** The Planner must load the `.mat` file and extract the initial conditions (input functions) and the full space-time solutions.

#### **2. Problem Statement**
- **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers Equation: $\partial u/\partial t + u \partial u/\partial x = \nu \partial^2 u/\partial x^2$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
- **Operator Mapping:** Mapping the initial condition $u(x, t=0)$ to the full trajectory $u(x, t)$.
- **Type:** This is a **Function-to-Trajectory** problem where $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

#### **3. Input ($F_{space}$)**
- **Domain:** 1D spatial domain $x \in [0, 1]$.
- **Value:** Scalar $u_0(x)$.
- **Structure:** Discretized grid of initial conditions.

#### **4. Output ($S_{space}$)**
- **Domain:** 2D space-time domain $(t, x) \in [0, 1] \times [0, 1]$.
- **Value:** Scalar $u(t, x)$.
- **Characteristics:** Periodic boundary conditions in $x$. The solution involves the formation and propagation of a shock, requiring high-fidelity feature capture.

#### **5. Normalization (In-Graph)**
- **Trunk (Spatial/Temporal):**
    - **Instruction:** The Planner must pass $(t, x)$ un-normalized. The model must normalize both $t$ and $x$ to **$[-1, 1]$** inside the `apply` function.
    - **Reason:** Periodic Fourier Features and `tanh` activation perform optimally in the $[-1, 1]$ range.
- **Branch (Input Function):**
    - **Instruction:** The Planner must pass $u_0(x)$ un-normalized. The model must normalize the input function to **$[-1, 1]$** inside the `apply` function using the global min/max of the training data.
    - **Reason:** Matches the `tanh` activation function.

#### **6. Architecture**

**Methodology:** `QR_DeepONet`

**6a. Branch Network**
- **Type:** Standard MLP.
- **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 1**.
    - **Layers:** `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]` (5 hidden layers + 1 embedding layer).
- **Activation:** `nn.tanh`.

**6b. Trunk Network**
- **Type:** Standard MLP with **Periodic Fourier Features**.
- **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 1**.
    - **Layers:** `[100, 100, 100, 100, 100, 120]`.
- **Activation:** `nn.tanh`.
- **Enhancements:** 
    - **Periodic Fourier Features:** Apply to the **spatial dimension ($x$)** of the trunk input *after* normalization to $[-1, 1]$.
    - **Parameters:** Set `kmax=10` and `L=2.0` (since the domain $[0, 1]$ is normalized to a range of 2).
    - **Instruction:** The temporal dimension ($t$) should remain as a raw normalized input or use standard polynomial embedding, but the spatial dimension **must** be transformed by the periodic basis to satisfy the user's BC requirement.

#### **7. Loss Function**
- **Type:** MSE (Standard for QR-DeepONet).
- **Lambda (vRBA):** 
    - **Instruction:** Use dual attention weights: `Lambda_spatial` (shape $N, P$) and `Lambda_latent` (shape $N, \text{embed\_dim}$).
- **Weights (W):** 
    - **Instruction:** Set $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$ to normalize the loss scale.

#### **8. Optimization & Hyperparameters**
- **Iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations = 200,000` (Standard for QR escalation).
- **Learning Rate:**
    - **Branch Optimizer:** `2e-3`.
    - **Trunk Optimizer:** `2e-3`.
- **Schedule:** `decay_rate = 0.99`, `decay_steps = 2500`.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE REQUIRED:**

**Main Model:**
- `QR_DeepONet`

**Enhancements:**
- `Periodic Fourier Features` (to be applied to the spatial component of the Trunk input).

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `Periodic_Fourier_Features` class to enforce periodic boundary conditions on the spatial input of the trunk network. This class maps the raw spatial coordinates into a high-dimensional periodic feature space using deterministic sine and cosine transformations based on the specified maximum wave number (`kmax`) and domain period (`Lx`). It is a standalone feature expansion module and does not rely on any other custom dependencies from the library.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### Periodic_Fourier_Features
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

```python
class QR_DeepONetModel(nn.Module): # LINE_2
    branch_layers: Sequence[int] # LINE_3
    trunk_layers: Sequence[int] # LINE_4
    N_fx: int # LINE_5
    f_min: float # LINE_5.1
    f_max: float # LINE_5.2
    kmax: int = 10 # LINE_5.3
    Lx: float = 2.0 # LINE_5.4
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh # LINE_6
    def setup(self): # LINE_7
        self.branch_net = MLP(features=self.branch_layers, # LINE_8
                              activation=self.activation) # LINE_9
        self.trunk_net = MLP(features=self.trunk_layers, # LINE_10
                             activation=self.activation) # LINE_11
        self.fourier_features = Periodic_Fourier_Features(kmax=self.kmax, Lx=self.Lx) # LINE_11.1
        embed_dim = self.trunk_layers[-1] # LINE_12
        A_init = jax.nn.initializers.variance_scaling( # LINE_13
            scale=1.0, mode='fan_in', distribution='truncated_normal' # LINE_14
        ) # LINE_15
        self.A = self.param('A', A_init, (embed_dim, self.N_fx)) # LINE_16
    def apply_stage1(self, y): # LINE_17
        y_norm = 2.0 * y - 1.0 # LINE_18
        t_norm = y_norm[:, 0:1] # LINE_18.1
        x_norm = y_norm[:, 1:2] # LINE_18.2
        x_fourier = self.fourier_features(x_norm) # LINE_18.3
        trunk_input = jnp.concatenate([t_norm, x_fourier], axis=1) # LINE_18.4
        Phi = self.trunk_net(trunk_input) # LINE_19
        return Phi, self.A # LINE_20
    def apply_stage2(self, F): # LINE_21
        F_norm = 2.0 * (F - self.f_min) / (self.f_max - self.f_min + 1e-8) - 1.0 # LINE_24
        C = self.branch_net(F_norm) # LINE_25
        return C # LINE_26
    def __call__(self, F, y): # LINE_28
        """ # LINE_29
        Defines the forward pass for PREDICTION/INFERENCE. # LINE_30
        This uses the QR-based prediction: U_pred = C @ Q.T # LINE_31
        """ # LINE_32
        C = self.apply_stage2(F) # LINE_37
        Phi, _ = self.apply_stage1(y) # LINE_38
        Q, _ = jnp.linalg.qr(Phi, mode='reduced') # LINE_39
        U_pred = C @ Q.T # LINE_40
        return U_pred # LINE_41
```

```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### **Scientific Strategy Report: QR-DeepONet with KKAN/eMLP for Viscous Burgers Equation**

#### **1. Data Source**
- **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
- **Instruction:** The Planner must verify the keys in the `.mat` file (typically `u`, `x`, `t` or similar) to ensure the initial condition $u(x, t=0)$ is correctly mapped as the Branch input and the full trajectory $u(x, t)$ as the target.

#### **2. Problem Statement**
- **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers Equation: $\partial_t u + u \partial_x u = \nu \partial_{xx} u$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
- **Operator Mapping:** Mapping the initial condition function $u_0(x)$ to the time-dependent solution $u(x, t)$.
- **Problem Type:** This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem where $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

#### **3. Input ($F_{space}$)**
- **Domain Dimension ($d$):** 1D ($x$).
- **Value Dimension ($k$):** Scalar ($u$).
- **Structure:** Grid-based discretization of the initial condition.

#### **4. Output ($S_{space}$)**
- **Domain Dimension ($d_y$):** 2D ($x, t$).
- **Value Dimension ($q$):** Scalar ($u$).
- **Characteristics:** Periodic boundary conditions. Low viscosity ($\nu=1000^{-1}$) suggests the potential for steep gradients or shock-like features that require high-capacity feature representation.

#### **5. Normalization (In-Graph)**
- **CRITICAL:** All normalization must be implemented *inside* the model's `__call__` method.
- **Trunk (KKAN):** The Planner must pass $(t, x)$ un-normalized. The model must normalize these to **$[0, 1]$** internally (required for RBF basis).
- **Branch (eMLP):** The Planner must pass the input function $u_0(x)$ un-normalized. The model must normalize this to **$[0, 1]$** internally.

#### **6. Architecture (Strategy 5: QR-DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch)**
- **Methodology:** `QR_DeepONet`
- **Branch:**
    - **Type:** `eMLP`
    - **Architecture:** Use `layers=[32, 32, 32, 101]`.
    - **Instruction:** Set `degree=7`.
- **Trunk:**
    - **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
    - **Outer Basis:** `RBF` (as recommended by the Advisor).
    - **Outer Degree:** 5.
    - **Inner eMLP Features:** `features=[32, 32, 32, 101]`.
    - **Inner Degree ($degree\_T$):** 5.
    - **Output Dimension:** 101 (must match Branch embedding).
- **Training Iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=200000`.

#### **7. Loss Function**
- **Type:** QR-DeepONet Loss.
- **Lambda (vRBA):** Implement dual attention: `Lambda_spatial` (shape $N, P$) and `Lambda_latent` (shape $N, 101$).
- **Error Function:** MSE.
- **Weights ($W$):** $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$.
- **Loss Formula:** `mean(W * (Lambda_spatial * (S_pred - S_true))**2)` combined with the latent projection loss inherent to QR-DeepONet.

#### **8. Optimization (Learning Rate)**
- **Branch Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `2e-3`.
- **Trunk Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to `6.66e-4` (approx `2e-3 / 3`).
- **Schedule:** Use `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500` for both optimizers.

### [Polynomial_Embedding]
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### [WN_layer]
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### [AdaptiveResNet]
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### [get_Psi]
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

### [RBF_KAN_layer]
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### [eMLP]
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### [KKAN]
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

Cell 6: Function Approximators (The "Swappable" Modules)
- Action: Replace `MLP` and `Periodic_Fourier_Features` (lines 2-22) with the `eMLP`, `KKAN`, and supporting layers (`Polynomial_Embedding`, `WN_layer`, `AdaptiveResNet`, `get_Psi`, `RBF_KAN_layer`) provided in [REFERENCE FUNCTIONS] to support the new architecture.
- New Code (lines 2-22):
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n

class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b

class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H

class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi

class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output

class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H

class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

--- 1. Calling Strategist Agent ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
### **Scientific Strategy Report: QR-DeepONet with KKAN (Fourier Basis) for Viscous Burgers Equation**

#### **1. Data Source**
- **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
- **Instruction:** The Planner must verify the keys in the `.mat` file (typically `u`, `x`, `t` or similar) and ensure the initial condition $u(x, t=0)$ is extracted as the Branch input.

#### **2. Problem Statement**
- **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers Equation: $\frac{\partial u}{\partial t} + u\frac{\partial u}{\partial x} = \nu \frac{\partial^2 u}{\partial x^2}$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
- **Operator Mapping:** This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem mapping the initial state $u(x, 0)$ to the full spatio-temporal solution $u(t, x)$.
- **Space Relation:** $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

#### **3. Input ($F_{space}$)**
- **Domain:** 1D spatial grid $x \in [0, 1]$.
- **Value:** Scalar velocity $u_0(x)$.
- **Structure:** Grid-based discretization of the initial condition.

#### **4. Output ($S_{space}$)**
- **Domain:** 2D spatio-temporal domain $(t, x) \in [0, 1] \times [0, 1]$.
- **Value:** Scalar velocity $u(t, x)$.
- **Characteristics:** Periodic boundary conditions. Low viscosity ($\nu=10^{-3}$) leads to the formation of sharp gradients (shocks), requiring high-precision capture of high-frequency features.

#### **5. Normalization (In-Graph)**
- **CRITICAL:** All normalization must be implemented *inside* the model's `__call__` method.
- **Trunk (t, x):** Instruct the Planner to normalize the coordinates $(t, x)$ to **$[-1, 1]$**. This is mandatory for the **Fourier basis** in the KKAN.
- **Branch ($u_0$):** Instruct the Planner to normalize the input function $u_0$ to **$[0, 1]$** (consistent with `eMLP` requirements).

#### **6. Architecture (Strategy 5: QR-DeepONet + KKAN/eMLP)**
- **Methodology:** `QR_DeepONet`
- **6a. Branch:**
    - **Type:** `eMLP`
    - **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 3**. Instruct the Planner to use `layers=[32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree=7`.
- **6b. Trunk:**
    - **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
    - **Outer Basis:** **`fourier`** (as recommended by the Advisor to match periodicity).
    - **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 3**. Instruct the Planner to set `output_dim=101` and `degree=5` (outer KAN). For the inner `eMLP` features, use `features=[32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree_T=5`.
- **Iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=200000`.

#### **7. Loss Function**
- **Type:** QR-specific MSE loss.
- **Lambda (vRBA):** Use dual attention: `Lambda_spatial` (shape $N, P$) and `Lambda_latent` (shape $N, \text{embed\_dim}$).
- **Weights ($W$):** $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$.
- **Formula:** `mean(W * (Lambda_spatial * (S_pred - S_true))**2)` + latent projection alignment.

#### **8. Optimization**
- **Branch Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to **`2e-3`**.
- **Trunk Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to **`6.66e-4`** (calculated as $2e-3 / 3$ for the KKAN).
- **Schedule:** Use the default decay: `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500`.

---

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE REQUIRED:**

**Main Model:**
- `QR_DeepONet`

**Enhancements:**
- `eMLP` (for Branch)
- `KKAN` (for Trunk)
- `KAN` with `fourier` basis (as the outer layer of KKAN)

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The `eMLP` and `KKAN` architectures rely on a shared set of foundational layers: `Polynomial_Embedding` for feature expansion, and `WN_layer` / `AdaptiveResNet` for the deep multi-layer perceptron core. `KKAN` specifically uses `get_Psi` to process each input dimension independently through an `eMLP`-like structure before summing them. The outer layer of `KKAN` then applies a KAN basis layer. 

Because the strategy requires a "fourier" basis for the outer KAN layer (which is not natively present in the library's KAN basis options), a `Fourier_KAN_layer` has been generated. The Planner will need to integrate this generated layer into the `KKAN` and `KAN` classes (e.g., replacing `RBF_KAN_layer` in `KKAN`'s `__call__` method). Note: The library defines `Polynomial_Embedding`, but `eMLP` and `get_Psi` call it as `Polynomial_Embedding_Layer`; the Planner will need to reconcile this naming difference.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Polynomial_Embedding]
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### [WN_layer]
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### [AdaptiveResNet]
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### [RBF_KAN_layer]
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### [Sin_KAN_layer]
```python
class Sin_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int = 5
    grid_min: float = -0.1
    grid_max: float = 0.1
    normalization: callable = jnp.sin ## This should not be changed!!! otherwise we need other normalization
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers=self.degree
        self.centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        X = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        p_i = self.centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)
        W_i = self.param(
            'W_i',
            nn.initializers.normal(1),
            (p_i.shape)
        )
        mu=jnp.exp(-(W_i**2)/2)*self.normalization_fn(p_i)
        sigma=jnp.sqrt(0.5-0.5*jnp.exp(-(2*W_i**2)*jnp.cos(2*p_i)-mu**2))+1e-12
        b_i=(self.normalization_fn(W_i*X+p_i)-mu)/sigma
        b_i = jnp.transpose(b_i, (0, 2, 1))
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', b_i, C_n)
        return output
```

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    num_centers: int = 5
    grid_min: float = -0.5
    grid_max: float = 0.5
    polynomial_type: str='T'
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        X = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        b = self.centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)
        W_p = self.param(
            'W_p',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (b.shape)
        )
        X=self.normalization_fn(W_p*X+b)
        X=jnp.sum(X,axis=1)
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                         (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # In,Out,Degree+1
        T_n= jnp.stack([self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(self.degree + 1)],axis=1)
        C_n_Tn = jnp.einsum("bdi,iod->bo", T_n, C_n)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [get_Psi]
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

### [eMLP]
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### [KKAN]
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

## GENERATED FUNCTION TREAT WITH CAUTION
### [Fourier_KAN_layer]
```python
class Fourier_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    normalization: callable = lambda x: x
    
    def setup(self):
        self.normalization_fn = self.normalization
        
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        
        # Frequencies k=1 to degree
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.degree + 1)
        
        # Phase for domain [-1, 1] (Lx=2) -> X * k * pi
        phase = X[:, :, None] * ks[None, None, :] * jnp.pi
        
        cos_terms = jnp.cos(phase)
        sin_terms = jnp.sin(phase)
        ones = jnp.ones((batch_size, in_dim, 1))
        
        # Concatenate to form basis: [1, cos(pi x), sin(pi x), ...]
        basis = jnp.concatenate([ones, cos_terms, sin_terms], axis=-1)
        
        num_features = 2 * self.degree + 1
        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * num_features)),
            (in_dim, num_features, self.out_dim)
        )
        
        # Einsum: b=batch, i=in_dim, f=features, o=out_dim
        output = jnp.einsum('bif,ifo->bo', basis, C_n)
        return output
```

### 0. Triage - Strategy Determination
*   **Specific Requests:** None found in `[USER_QUESTION]`.
*   **Advisor Recommendation:** The Advisor noted that Run 13 (QR-DeepONet + KKAN/eMLP + Fourier) plateaued at 5.70%. The recommendation is to **escalate from QR to SVD decomposition** while maintaining the successful KKAN/eMLP architecture and Fourier basis.
*   **Path:** Path 2 (Advisor Recommendation). I will implement **SVD-DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch**.

### 1. Data Source
*   **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
*   **Instruction:** The Planner must verify the keys in the `.mat` file (likely `u`, `x`, `t`, `usol`).

### 2. Problem Statement
*   **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers Equation: $\partial u/\partial t + u \partial u/\partial x = \nu \partial^2 u/\partial x^2$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
*   **Operator Mapping:** Mapping the initial condition $u(x, t=0)$ to the full spatio-temporal solution $u(x, t)$.
*   **Type:** This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem where $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

### 3. Input ($F_{space}$)
*   **Domain Dimension ($d$):** 1D ($x$).
*   **Value Dimension ($k$):** Scalar ($u_0$).
*   **Structure:** Grid-based initial conditions.

### 4. Output ($S_{space}$)
*   **Domain Dimension ($d_y$):** 2D ($t, x$).
*   **Value Dimension ($q$):** Scalar ($u$).
*   **Characteristics:** Periodic boundary conditions, high-gradient features (shocks) developing over time.

### 5. Normalization (In-Graph)
*   **Trunk:** Instruct the Planner to normalize the $(t, x)$ coordinates to **$[-1, 1]$** inside the model's `apply` function. This is required for the **Fourier basis** in the KKAN.
*   **Branch:** Instruct the Planner to normalize the input function $u_0(x)$ to **$[0, 1]$** inside the model's `apply` function. This is consistent with the **eMLP** architecture.

### 6. Architecture

**6.1. Methodology:**
*   **Type:** `SVD_DeepONet`. This replaces the QR decomposition with Singular Value Decomposition to extract optimal basis modes.

**6.2. Branch Network:**
*   **Type:** `eMLP`.
*   **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 3**. Instruct the Planner to use `layers=[32, 32, 32, 101]` and `degree=7`.
*   **Activation:** `nn.relu` (internal to eMLP).

**6.3. Trunk Network:**
*   **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
*   **Architecture:** Use **Sizing Rule 3**.
    *   **Outer KAN:** `output_dim=101`, `degree=5`.
    *   **Inner eMLPs:** `features=[32, 32, 32, 101]`, `degree_T=5`.
*   **Basis:** `fourier` (to handle the periodic boundary conditions).

**6.4. Training Parameters:**
*   **Iterations:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations=200000` (Standard for SVD/QR models).

### 7. Loss Function
*   **Type:** SVD-specific MSE.
*   **Lambda (vRBA):** Use dual attention: $\Lambda_{spatial}$ (size $N \times P$) and $\Lambda_{latent}$ (size $N \times \text{embed\_dim}$).
*   **Weights ($W$):** $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$.
*   **Formula:** `mean(W * (Lambda_spatial * (S_pred - S_true))**2)` + latent space constraints inherent to SVD-DeepONet.

### 8. Optimization (Learning Rate)
*   **Branch Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to **`2e-3`**.
*   **Trunk Optimizer:** Instruct the Planner to set the initial learning rate to **`6.66e-4`** (calculated as $2e-3 / 3$ for the KKAN).
*   **Schedule:** Use the default decay: `decay_rate = 0.99` and `decay_steps = 2500`.

--- 3. Retrieving auxiliary functions ---
**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `eMLP` and `KKAN` architectures, along with a Fourier basis for the KKAN. The `eMLP` and `KKAN` models rely heavily on shared architectural components: `WN_layer` (Weight Normalization), `AdaptiveResNet` (Adaptive Residual Connections), and `Polynomial_Embedding_Layer` (renamed from `Polynomial_Embedding` in the library to match the internal calls within `eMLP` and `get_Psi`). The `KKAN` model uses `get_Psi` to process the inner functions and an outer KAN layer to combine them. 

Since the library lacks a dedicated `Fourier_KAN_layer` for the outer KAN block, I have generated one using the `RBF_KAN_layer` as a template. I have also included `Periodic_Fourier_Features` in case the Planner prefers to use it for input feature expansion. `RBF_KAN_layer` is included as a dependency because the default `KKAN` class references it.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### WN_layer
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### AdaptiveResNet
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### Polynomial_Embedding_Layer
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### Periodic_Fourier_Features
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### RBF_KAN_layer
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

## GENERATED FUNCTION TREAT WITH CAUTION
### Fourier_KAN_layer
```python
class Fourier_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    normalization: callable = lambda x: x
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization(X)
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        
        # Frequencies
        k = jnp.arange(1, self.degree + 1)
        
        # Compute basis: [1, cos(pi*k*x), sin(pi*k*x)]
        X_expanded = X[:, :, None] * k[None, None, :] # (batch, in_dim, degree)
        cos_terms = jnp.cos(jnp.pi * X_expanded)
        sin_terms = jnp.sin(jnp.pi * X_expanded)
        
        # Concatenate along the last axis
        ones = jnp.ones((batch_size, in_dim, 1))
        basis = jnp.concatenate([ones, cos_terms, sin_terms], axis=-1) # (batch, in_dim, 2*degree + 1)
        
        # Trainable coefficients
        num_basis = 2 * self.degree + 1
        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * num_basis)),
            (in_dim, self.out_dim, num_basis)
        )
        
        # Combine basis with coefficients
        output = jnp.einsum('bid,iod->bo', basis, C_n)
        return output
```

### eMLP
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### get_Psi
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

### KKAN
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```

- New Code (lines 3-17):
```python
class KKAN_Fourier(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    degree_T: int = 5
    output_dim: int = 1
    def setup(self):
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPsi function (2D input: t, x)
        inputs = [X[:, 0:1], X[:, 1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = Fourier_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi

class DeepONetModel(nn.Module):
    branch_layers: Sequence[int]
    branch_degree: int
    trunk_inner_features: Sequence[int]
    trunk_inner_degree: int
    trunk_outer_degree: int
    trunk_output_dim: int

    def setup(self):
        # Branch: eMLP (Section 6.2)
        self.branch_net = eMLP(layers=self.branch_layers, 
                               degree=self.branch_degree)
        
        # Trunk: KKAN with Fourier Basis (Section 6.3)
        self.trunk_net = KKAN_Fourier(features=self.trunk_inner_features, 
                                      degree=self.trunk_outer_degree, 
                                      degree_T=self.trunk_inner_degree, 
                                      output_dim=self.trunk_output_dim)

    def __call__(self, F, y):
        # --- Normalization (Section 5) ---
        # Branch: Normalize input function to [0, 1]
        F = (F - jnp.min(F)) / (jnp.max(F) - jnp.min(F) + 1e-8)
        # Trunk: Normalize coordinates to [-1, 1]
        y_min = jnp.min(y, axis=0)
        y_max = jnp.max(y, axis=0)
        y = 2.0 * (y - y_min) / (y_max - y_min + 1e-8) - 1.0

        # --- Forward Pass ---
        B = self.branch_net(F) # Coefficients
        T = self.trunk_net(y).T # Basis functions
        
        U_pred = B @ T
        return U_pred
```

```python
# Cell 7: Initialize Model and Optimizer

                      branch_degree=branch_degree,
                      trunk_inner_features=trunk_inner_features,
                      trunk_inner_degree=trunk_inner_degree,
                      trunk_outer_degree=trunk_outer_degree,
                      trunk_output_dim=trunk_output_dim)

itercount = itertools.count()
apply_fn = jax.jit(model.apply)
```

Cell 10: The vRBA-Weighted Loss Function
- Action: Strategist (Section 7) requires an SVD-specific MSE loss with dual attention ($\Lambda_{spatial}$ and $\Lambda_{latent}$) and a global weight $W$.
- Action: Modify `loss_all` to compute residuals in both the spatial domain ($S_{pred} - S_{true}$) and the latent space ($B - S_{latent\_true}$).
- Action: Update both sets of vRBA weights using the provided baked functions.
- Action: Implement the weighted loss formula from Section 7: `mean(W * (Lambda * R)**2)`.
- Action: Return the total loss and a tuple containing both updated weight batches.

- New Code (lines 3-10):
```python
def loss_all(params, model, F, y, S_true, S_latent_true, Lambda_spatial_batch, Lambda_latent_batch, it, current_gamma, eta, 
             spatial_update_fn_baked, latent_update_fn_baked, W):
    """
    SVD-specific MSE loss with dual attention (Section 7).
    """
    # 1. Predict: Get coefficients (B) and full reconstruction (S_pred)
    # We use the sub-networks directly to get the latent representation
    B = model.apply(params, F, method=lambda self, x: self.branch_net(x))
    T = model.apply(params, y, method=lambda self, x: self.trunk_net(x)).T
    S_pred = B @ T
    
    # 2. Get Residuals
    R_spatial = S_pred - S_true
    R_latent = B - S_latent_true
    
    # 3. Update Weights (Dual Attention)
    new_Lambda_spatial = spatial_update_fn_baked(R_spatial, Lambda_spatial_batch, it, current_gamma, eta)
    new_Lambda_latent = latent_update_fn_baked(R_latent, Lambda_latent_batch, it, current_gamma, eta)
    
    # 4. Compute Weighted Loss (Section 7 formula)
    loss_spatial = jnp.mean(W * (new_Lambda_spatial * R_spatial)**2)
    loss_latent = jnp.mean(W * (new_Lambda_latent * R_latent)**2)
    
    total_loss = loss_spatial + loss_latent
    
    return total_loss, (new_Lambda_spatial, new_Lambda_latent)
```

- New Code (lines 4-14):
```python
@partial(jit, static_argnums=(1, 2, 12, 13)) 
def compute_grads_and_loss(params, loss_fn, model,  
                           F, y, S_true, S_latent_true, Lambda_spatial_batch, Lambda_latent_batch, 
                           it, current_gamma, eta, 
                           spatial_update_fn_baked, latent_update_fn_baked, W):
     
    def loss_wrapper(p):
        return loss_fn(p, model, F, y, S_true, S_latent_true, 
                       Lambda_spatial_batch, Lambda_latent_batch, it,  
                       current_gamma, eta, spatial_update_fn_baked, 
                       latent_update_fn_baked, W)
    
    (loss_value, new_Lambda_batches), g = jax.value_and_grad(loss_wrapper, has_aux=True)(params)
     
    return loss_value, new_Lambda_batches, g
```

- New Code (lines 2-11):
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer
    def setup(self):
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b

class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H

class Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n

class Fourier_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    normalization: callable = lambda x: x
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization(X)
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        k = jnp.arange(1, self.degree + 1)
        X_expanded = X[:, :, None] * k[None, None, :]
        cos_terms = jnp.cos(jnp.pi * X_expanded)
        sin_terms = jnp.sin(jnp.pi * X_expanded)
        ones = jnp.ones((batch_size, in_dim, 1))
        basis = jnp.concatenate([ones, cos_terms, sin_terms], axis=-1)
        num_basis = 2 * self.degree + 1
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * num_basis)), (in_dim, self.out_dim, num_basis))
        output = jnp.einsum('bid,iod->bo', basis, C_n)
        return output

class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(x)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H

class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            X_emb = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(X)
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X_emb))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

```python
            params, states[key] = update_model( # LINE_49
                key, optimizers[key], gradient, params, states[key] # LINE_50
            ) # LINE_51
        Lambda_global = Lambda_global.at[idx_train].set(new_Lambda_batch) # LINE_52
        Lambda_latent_global = Lambda_latent_global.at[idx_train].set(new_Lambda_latent_batch) # LINE_53
```

Cell 10: The vRBA-Weighted Loss Function
- Action: Fix the `ValueError` identified in the [PREVIOUS_ERROR_REPORT] by replacing the `model` (Flax Module) argument with `apply_fn` (the functional apply method). Flax Modules are not hashable and cannot be used as static arguments in JIT-compiled functions.
- Action: Update the internal calls to use `apply_fn` instead of `model.apply`.
- New Code (lines 2-17):
```python
def loss_all(params, apply_fn, F, y, S_true, S_latent_true, Lambda_spatial_batch, Lambda_latent_batch, it, current_gamma, eta, 
             spatial_update_fn_baked, latent_update_fn_baked, W):
    """
    SVD-specific MSE loss with dual attention (Section 7).
    """
    # Use apply_fn with method lambdas to access sub-networks
    B = apply_fn(params, F, method=lambda self, x: self.branch_net(x))
    T = apply_fn(params, y, method=lambda self, x: self.trunk_net(x)).T
    
    S_pred = B @ T
    R_spatial = S_pred - S_true
    R_latent = B - S_latent_true
    
    new_Lambda_spatial = spatial_update_fn_baked(R_spatial, Lambda_spatial_batch, it, current_gamma, eta)
    new_Lambda_latent = latent_update_fn_baked(R_latent, Lambda_latent_batch, it, current_gamma, eta)
    
    loss_spatial = jnp.mean(W * (new_Lambda_spatial * R_spatial)**2)
    loss_latent = jnp.mean(W * (new_Lambda_latent * R_latent)**2)
    
    total_loss = loss_spatial + loss_latent
    return total_loss, (new_Lambda_spatial, new_Lambda_latent)
```

    - Replacing lines 2-17 with 21 new lines.

--- Processing: Cell 11 ---

Cell 11: Gradient and Update Functions
- Action: Fix the `ValueError` from the [PREVIOUS_ERROR_REPORT] by replacing the `model` argument with `apply_fn` in `compute_grads_and_loss`. Flax Modules are unhashable and cannot be used as static arguments in JIT.
- Action: Update the `loss_wrapper` to pass `apply_fn` to the loss function, matching the signature change made in Cell 10.
- New Code (lines 2-13):
```python
@partial(jit, static_argnums=(1, 2, 12, 13))
def compute_grads_and_loss(params, loss_fn, apply_fn,
    F, y, S_true, S_latent_true, Lambda_spatial_batch, Lambda_latent_batch,
    it, current_gamma, eta,
    spatial_update_fn_baked, latent_update_fn_baked, W):
    def loss_wrapper(p):
        return loss_fn(p, apply_fn, F, y, S_true, S_latent_true,
            Lambda_spatial_batch, Lambda_latent_batch, it,
            current_gamma, eta, spatial_update_fn_baked,
            latent_update_fn_baked, W)
    (loss_value, new_Lambda_batches), g = jax.value_and_grad(loss_wrapper, has_aux=True)(params)
    return loss_value, new_Lambda_batches, g
```

Cell 10: The vRBA-Weighted Loss Function
- Action: Strategist (Section 5) ordered in-graph normalization for Branch and Trunk.
- Action: Adapt `loss_all` to include the normalization logic from Cell 5 to ensure consistency when calling sub-networks directly via `method`.
- Action: Replace the existing `loss_all` implementation (lines 7-21) with the normalized version.
- New Code (lines 7-21):
```python
    # Normalization (matching Cell 5 logic)
    F_norm = (F - jnp.min(F)) / (jnp.max(F) - jnp.min(F) + 1e-8)
    y_min = jnp.min(y, axis=0)
    y_max = jnp.max(y, axis=0)
    y_norm = 2.0 * (y - y_min) / (y_max - y_min + 1e-8) - 1.0
    
    # Use apply_fn with method lambdas to access sub-networks
    B = apply_fn(params, F_norm, method=lambda self, x: self.branch_net(x))
    T = apply_fn(params, y_norm, method=lambda self, x: self.trunk_net(x)).T
     
    S_pred = B @ T
    R_spatial = S_pred - S_true
    R_latent = B - S_latent_true
     
    new_Lambda_spatial = spatial_update_fn_baked(R_spatial, Lambda_spatial_batch, it, current_gamma, eta)
    new_Lambda_latent = latent_update_fn_baked(R_latent, Lambda_latent_batch, it, current_gamma, eta)
     
    loss_spatial = jnp.mean(W * (new_Lambda_spatial * R_spatial)**2)
    loss_latent = jnp.mean(W * (new_Lambda_latent * R_latent)**2)
     
    total_loss = loss_spatial + loss_latent
    return total_loss, (new_Lambda_spatial, new_Lambda_latent)
```

```python
    contour1 = ax.contourf(XX, TT, S_pred, levels=100, cmap='jet')
    fig.colorbar(contour1, ax=ax)
    ax.set_title(f' Prediction (Sample {sample_idx})')
    ax.set_xlabel('x')
    ax.set_ylabel('t')
    
    ax = axes[row_idx, 1]
    contour2 = ax.contourf(XX, TT, S_test, levels=100, cmap='jet')
    fig.colorbar(contour2, ax=ax)
    ax.set_title(f' Reference (Sample {sample_idx})')
    ax.set_xlabel('x')
    ax.set_ylabel('t')
    
    ax = axes[row_idx, 2]
    contour3 = ax.contourf(XX, TT, pointwise_error, levels=100, cmap='jet')
    fig.colorbar(contour3, ax=ax)
    ax.set_title(f'Error (Sample {sample_idx})')
    ax.set_xlabel('x')
    ax.set_ylabel('t')
```

--- Strategy Report Received ---
**Scientific Strategy Report: Refinement of QR-DeepONet for Viscous Burgers Equation**

### 0. Triage & Strategy Selection
*   **Analysis:** The previous attempt (Run 18: SVD-DeepONet) failed significantly with a 51.3% error. Following the **Advisor's Recommendation**, we are reverting to the best-performing historical model: **Run 13**.
*   **Selected Strategy:** **Strategy 5 (QR-DeepONet + KKAN Trunk / eMLP Branch)**.
*   **Refinement:** Per the Advisor, we will double the training iterations to push the error below the 5% threshold.

### 1. Data Source
*   **Path:** `/oscar/data/gk/jdtoscan/References/ATHENA_Agentic_Team_for_Hierarchical_Evolutionary_Numerical_Algorithms/Documents/codes/Data/Burger_1000_5000.mat`
*   **Instruction:** The Planner must verify the keys in the `.mat` file (typically `u`, `x`, `t`) and ensure the mapping from $u(x, t=0)$ to $u(x, t)$ is correctly extracted.

### 2. Problem Statement
*   **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers Equation: $\frac{\partial u}{\partial t} + u\frac{\partial u}{\partial x} = \nu \frac{\partial^2 u}{\partial x^2}$ with $\nu = 0.001$.
*   **Operator Mapping:** Mapping the initial condition $u_0(x)$ to the full spatio-temporal solution $u(t, x)$.
*   **Type:** This is a Function-to-Trajectory problem where $F_{space} \neq S_{space}$.

### 3. Input ($F_{space}$)
*   **Domain Dimension ($d$):** 1D ($x$).
*   **Value Dimension ($k$):** Scalar ($u_0$).
*   **Structure:** Discretized grid of initial conditions.

### 4. Output ($S_{space}$)
*   **Domain Dimension ($d_y$):** 2D ($t, x$).
*   **Value Dimension ($q$):** Scalar ($u$).
*   **Characteristics:** Periodic boundary conditions. Low viscosity ($\nu=1000^{-1}$) leads to steep gradients and shock-like features, requiring high-expressivity basis functions.

### 5. Normalization (In-Graph)
*   **CRITICAL INSTRUCTION:** All normalization must be implemented *inside* the model's `apply` function.
*   **Trunk ($t, x$):** Normalize coordinates to **$[-1, 1]$** (required for the Fourier basis in KKAN).
*   **Branch ($u_0$):** Normalize the input function to **$[0, 1]$** (required for the eMLP architecture).
*   **Activations:** Use `nn.tanh` for the internal MLP components of the eMLP/KKAN to maintain consistency with the best-performing Run 13.

### 6. Architecture (Strategy 5: QR-DeepONet + KKAN/eMLP)
*   **Methodology:** `QR_DeepONet` (utilizing QR decomposition for the latent basis).
*   **6a. Branch Network:**
    *   **Type:** `eMLP`
    *   **Architecture:** `layers=[32, 32, 32, 101]`, `degree=7`.
    *   **Instruction:** The final dimension (101) must match the Trunk's output dimension.
*   **6b. Trunk Network:**
    *   **Type:** `KKAN` (Kurkova Kolmogorov-Arnold Network).
    *   **Basis:** **Fourier Basis** (as specified in Run 13).
    *   **Architecture:** `output_dim=101`, `degree=5` (outer KAN).
    *   **Inner Features:** `features=[32, 32, 32, 101]`, `degree_T=5`.
*   **Iterations:** **CRITICAL:** Instruct the Planner to set `max_iterations = 400,000` (doubling the standard 200k for QR models to ensure convergence).

### 7. Loss Function
*   **Type:** QR-specific MSE.
*   **Lambda (vRBA):** Use dual attention weights: $\Lambda_{spatial}$ (size $N \times P$) and $\Lambda_{latent}$ (size $N \times \text{embed\_dim}$).
*   **Weights ($W$):** $W = 1.0 / \max(|S|)$ to normalize the scale of the Burgers solution.

### 8. Optimization (Learning Rate)
*   **Branch Optimizer:** Initial `learning_rate = 2e-3`.
*   **Trunk Optimizer:** Initial `learning_rate = 6.66e-4` (approx $2e-3 / 3$).
*   **Schedule:** `decay_rate = 0.99`, `decay_steps = 2500`.

The Planner will need to implement `vRBA` directly or proceed with standard QR-DeepONet attention.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Polynomial_Embedding]
```python
class Polynomial_Embedding(nn.Module):   
    degree: int
    step: int = 1 
    polynomial_type: str ='T'
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(0, self.degree+1, self.step)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        C_n = self.param('c_i', nn.initializers.ones, (len(self.T_funcs)))  # Adjust size based on the step
        C_n_T_n = jnp.hstack([C_n[i] / (i + 1) * self.T_funcs[i](X) for i in range(len(self.T_funcs))])
        return C_n_T_n
```

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### [WN_layer]
```python
class WN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_features: int  # Number of output features
    kernel_init: nn.initializers.Initializer  # Custom initializer for W
    def setup(self):
        # Define bias and scale parameters; W will be initialized later
        self.b = self.param('b', nn.initializers.zeros, (self.out_features,))
        self.g = self.param('g', nn.initializers.ones, (self.out_features,))
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        # Determine input size from H dynamically
        in_features = H.shape[-1]
        # Initialize W with the specified kernel initializer and dynamic shape
        W = self.param('W', self.kernel_init, (in_features, self.out_features))
        # Weight normalization
        V = W / jnp.linalg.norm(W, axis=0, keepdims=True)
        return self.g * jnp.dot(H, V) + self.b
```

### [AdaptiveResNet]
```python
class AdaptiveResNet(nn.Module):
    out_features: int
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize alpha as a trainable parameter with a single value
        self.alpha = self.param('alpha', nn.initializers.ones, ())
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, H):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        F = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(H))
        G = WN_layer(self.out_features, kernel_init=init)(F)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(self.alpha * G + (1 - self.alpha) * H)
        return H
```

### [RBF_KAN_layer]
```python
class RBF_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    grid_min: float = -2.0
    grid_max: float = 2.0
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.num_centers = self.degree
        self.sigma = (self.grid_max - self.grid_min) / (self.num_centers - 1)
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = X # It is possible to add normalization self.normalization_fn(X) but is generally not needed
        batch_size, in_dim = X.shape
        centers = self.param(
            'centers',
            lambda rng, shape: jnp.linspace(self.grid_min, self.grid_max, self.num_centers),
            (self.num_centers,)
        )

        X_expanded = X[:, None, :]             # Shape: (batch_size, 1, in_dim)
        centers_expanded = centers[None, :, None]  # Shape: (1, num_centers, 1)

        diff = X_expanded - centers_expanded   # Shape: (batch_size, num_centers, in_dim)

        RBF_n = jnp.exp(- (diff ** 2) / (2 * self.sigma ** 2))

        RBF_n = jnp.transpose(RBF_n, (0, 2, 1))

        C_n = self.param(
            'C_n',
            nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * self.num_centers)),
            (in_dim, self.num_centers, self.out_dim)
        )
        output = jnp.einsum('bin,ino->bo', RBF_n, C_n)

        return output
```

### [get_Psi]
```python
class get_Psi(nn.Module):
    degree: int
    features: Sequence[int]
    M: int = 10
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"T{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, inputs):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        sum_psi = 0
        
        # Loop over each input dimension (e.g., x_1, x_2, ...)
        for i, X in enumerate(inputs):
            # Each input is processed by its OWN eMLP
            
            # 1. Input Embedding
            X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(X)
            # 2. Main MLP Body
            H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.features[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
            for fs in self.features[1:-1]:
                H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=fs)(H) 
            # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
            H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
            # 4. Final Layer
            H = WN_layer(self.features[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
            # 5. Accumulate results (the \sum_p part)
            sum_psi += H
        return sum_psi
```

### [eMLP]
```python
class eMLP(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    activation: Callable = nn.relu
    degree: int=5
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        init = nn.initializers.glorot_normal()
        # 1. Input Embedding
        X = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(x)
        
        # 2. Main MLP Body (e.g., with ResNets)
        H = nn.activation.tanh(WN_layer(self.layers[0], kernel_init=init)(X))
        for feat in self.layers[1:-1]:
            H = AdaptiveResNet(out_features=feat)(H) 
        # 3. Pre-Output Embedding
        H = Polynomial_Embedding_Layer(degree=self.degree)(H)
        
        # 4. Final Layer
        H = WN_layer(self.layers[-1], kernel_init=init)(H)
        return H
```

### [KKAN]
```python
class KKAN(nn.Module):
    features: Sequence[int]
    degree: int=5
    degree_T: int=5
    M: int = 10
    output_dim: int =1
    activation: Callable = nn.tanh
    def setup(self):
        # Initialize the GetPhi submodule
        self.get_Psi = get_Psi(degree=self.degree_T, features=self.features, M=self.M)
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        # Process inputs through the GetPhi function
        inputs = [X[:,0:1],X[:,1:2]]
        sum_psi = self.get_Psi(inputs)
        sum_Phi = RBF_KAN_layer(out_dim=self.output_dim, degree=self.degree)(sum_psi)
        return sum_Phi
```
